/**
 *  v3.00.0 — FR-28 (s140): GET /api/join-trip/:code — the trip twin of
 *            /api/join-round/:code, so Pards Lite can resolve a trip foursome
 *            code. ADDITIVE ONLY: one new route, one new handler, nothing
 *            existing touched. The trip SCORE rail already existed (v2.92.0).
 *  v2.99.0 — B-121 (s135): recomputeLeaderboard no longer writes a group-shaped
 *            cache for an event whose scores are not addressed to its groups.
 *            scores.group_id is POLYMORPHIC — the classic group-code writer puts
 *            groups.id in it, the tee-time foursome writer puts tee_times.id —
 *            so the scan's join matched nothing on every multi-day event and
 *            cached holes_played:0 against fully scored rounds. Now it computes
 *            NOTHING there and clears the stale row. ⛔ Deliberately NOT a join
 *            repair: see the block comment in recomputeLeaderboard.
 *            REMOVAL ONLY · no schema change · no new response field.
 *  v2.98.0 — FR-42 §E (s134): GET /api/events/:eventId/rounds/:roundId/money.
 *            Read twin of the v2.93.0 POST, same JOIN-players projection the
 *            trip GET uses since v2.97.0. Lets the EVENT-scoped Game Results
 *            report show a round's side games without reading trip state.
 *            Read-only · no schema change · every failure path returns [].
 *  v2.97.0 — B-116 (s133). TRIP SIDE-GAME MONEY INVISIBLE FOR ~10 MIN.
 *            Trip side-game money posted from a phone did not appear in Trip
 *            Money on any OTHER device for up to 10 minutes. No money was
 *            ever lost or wrong -- visibility only, self-healing on cache
 *            expiry or reload.
 *            Cause is client-side but the fix belongs here. _ptTrpLedger
 *            bridges a money row to a player NAME via gdd.pidName, which is
 *            rebuilt from a SEPARATE scores fetch cached for 10 minutes
 *            (_ptTrpGDCache). A row whose name is missing is dropped
 *            silently. B-94's two cache busts only fire on the device that
 *            made the change, so a second device pairs fresh money with a
 *            stale name bridge and drops every row.
 *            Fix: the trip GET now carries the name ON the money row itself
 *            -- JOIN players p ON p.id = erm.player_id, selecting
 *            p.name AS player_name. The client prefers m.player_name and
 *            keeps pidName only as a fallback, so the name no longer depends
 *            on a cache the posting device never invalidated.
 *            One added JOIN, one added column. No DDL, no new endpoint, no
 *            behaviour change for any other caller.
 *            Client half required: v466.0 (_ptTrpLedger + the sibling filter
 *            at the second call site both read m.player_name first).
 *
 *  v2.96.0 — D86 (s123). THE FOLD GATE WAS THE WRONG WORD.
 *            tripEventRail decided whether a game-day round contributes points
 *            by testing the FORMAT NAME for "stableford". The scramble family
 *            is correctly skipped above it (shared ball = no individual card),
 *            and low-wins formats correctly fold at 0 by the v2.69.0 bind. But
 *            own-ball TEAM formats -- team_ccc123w_4_net, team_2of4_net -- match
 *            NEITHER branch. They fell through to pts: 0 while still being fully
 *            scored, 18 real holes per man. On trip 56 Scottsdale, whose only
 *            round is Kierland (team_ccc123w_4_net), that produced 12 players,
 *            12 zeroes and a 12-way tie for first.
 *            Fix: the gate is now OWN BALL, not the word. isLowWins folds at 0
 *            exactly as before; everything else that survived the scramble skip
 *            folds at the points already computed into pts_raw. Two formats
 *            change behaviour, both own-ball team formats, both the intended
 *            targets. No new query, no new column, no schema change.
 *            ⭐ B-102/B-104 are NOT here -- both are client-side. The payload
 *            already carries game_rounds (v2.94.0); the client just ignores it.
 *
 *  v2.95.0 — B-100 (s122). THE ROSTER LINK WAS ROTTING.
 *            v2.94.0 reads players.roster_id but NOTHING EVER WROTE IT.
 *            All three INSERT INTO players sites omitted the column, so the
 *            s121 backfill was a one-time snapshot and every player created
 *            afterwards landed NULL -- i.e. folded nowhere. Caught live: all
 *            12 players of event 113 "Day One - Kierland" (trip 56) were NULL.
 *            Fix: at BOTH sites the roster id is already in hand (it is written
 *            to roster_event_links two lines later), so we now also stamp it
 *            onto players.roster_id. Additive, guarded by "AND roster_id IS
 *            NULL" so it never overwrites a deliberate value, and wrapped
 *            fail-soft so a pre-migration DB cannot break scoring.
 *            NO NAME MATCHING -- the standing rule from s121 holds.
 *
 *  v2.94.0 — FR-31 + FR-32 (s121).
 *            1. FR-31: handleTripLeaderboard now spans BOTH rails. Casual
 *               trip_rounds and game-day event rounds (via trip_events) fold
 *               into ONE overall standing. New helper tripEventRail().
 *               ⭐ Join is players.roster_id -> trip_players.roster_id ONLY.
 *               NO NAME MATCHING. A NULL roster_id is a MISS and is counted
 *               in `unlinked_players` so the client can say "N not linked".
 *               ⭐ v2.69.0 BIND HELD: low-wins game rounds add 0 to total_pts
 *               and post to low_boards only. The sort is UNTOUCHED.
 *               Requires the additive column added in s121:
 *                 ALTER TABLE players ADD COLUMN roster_id INTEGER REFERENCES roster(id)
 *               Worker executes NO DDL. Fail-soft: any error on the game-day
 *               rail returns an EMPTY rail, never breaks the casual board.
 *            2. FR-32 "🎲 No games — just golf": handleCreateRound accepts an
 *               explicit format:'' instead of coercing it to stableford.

 *  v2.93.0 — FR-26 phase 1 (s119): event_round_money write/read + trip GET carry.
 *            1. New table event_round_money (created by SEPARATE migration —
 *               already run on live D1; this worker executes NO DDL). Exact
 *               twin of trip_round_money keyed (round_id, player_id),
 *               UNIQUE(round_id, player_id), FK round_id ON DELETE CASCADE.
 *            2. POST /api/events/:eventId/rounds/:roundId/money
 *               {group_code, money:[{player_id, cents, detail?}]} — twin of
 *               handleSubmitTripRoundMoney on the EVENT rail: group_code is
 *               the scores rail's tee_time_groups.code (uppercased), must
 *               belong to THIS round; players validated against THIS event.
 *               Upsert per (round, player): a re-save REPLACES, never
 *               doubles (D83).
 *            3. GET /api/trips/:id carries event_round_money[] — game-day
 *               money — via the same trip_events linkage game_days[] uses;
 *               degrades to [] pre-migration exactly like round_money.
 *            4. Cascades: handleDeleteEvent and handleDeleteRound sweep
 *               event_round_money explicitly (FK CASCADE is the backstop).

 *  v2.92.0 — D84 + FR-25 (s116): the trips clean-slate worker half.
 *            1. D84: trip_events link table (created by SEPARATE migration —
 *               this worker executes NO DDL). New routes, cloned from the
 *               D82 league pair:
 *                 POST   /api/trips/:id/events       {event_id} → link (409 on dup)
 *                 DELETE /api/trips/:id/events/:eid  → unlink ONLY, never the event
 *               GET /api/trips/:id gains game_days: [...] BESIDE rounds
 *               (degrades to [] pre-migration), and the events LIST gains
 *               trip_id per event (falls back to the v2.91.0 SELECT
 *               pre-migration) so the client hides game-day events from the
 *               TB Events list in O(1) — no N+1 fan-out.
 *            2. FR-25: trip_round_money (same migration) + code-authenticated
 *               POST /api/trips/:id/rounds/:rid/money {foursome_code, money[]}
 *               — upsert per (round, player): a re-save REPLACES, never
 *               doubles (D83: the ledger reports what the games computed, it
 *               never invents money). Carried on GET /api/trips/:id as
 *               round_money[].

 *  v2.91.0 — B-84 (round lock compares, not presence-tests) + D82
 *            (league_events team-night link endpoints).
 *            1. B-84: handleUpdateRound's D1 lock on course_id/tee/format
 *               refused any PATCH that merely CONTAINED those keys once the
 *               round had posted scores — an old client echoing unchanged
 *               values 409'd a date-only edit. The lock now compares sent vs
 *               stored (String(sent||'') !== String(stored||''), matching the
 *               client's diff) and only a REAL change on a scored round 409s,
 *               with the same error message.
 *            2. D82: league_events link table (created by SEPARATE migration —
 *               this worker executes NO DDL). New routes:
 *                 POST   /api/leagues/:id/events            {event_id} — must
 *                        own BOTH league and event; UNIQUE violation → 409.
 *                 DELETE /api/leagues/:id/events/:event_id  — UNLINK ONLY,
 *                        never cascades to the event.
 *               GET /api/leagues/:id gains team_nights: [...] BESIDE rounds
 *               (never merged); degrades to [] pre-migration, so this file is
 *               safe to paste before OR after the migration runs.
 *               handleLeagueSeason: zero diff (joins league_rounds.trip_id
 *               only; league_events appears nowhere in it).

 *  v2.90.0 — ⭐⭐ B-82 (worker half) + B-79 (reset-epoch) + THE MATCH_PLAY /
 *            BEST_BALL LATCH (session 108).
 *            1. B-82: handleWatch's round pick is now ACTIVE → LOCKED → DRAFT,
 *               then highest round_number. The old B-32 rule let an empty DRAFT
 *               outrank the watched round twice on live data (event 95 round 3
 *               over ACTIVE round 1; event 96 draft 80 over LOCKED money round
 *               79). ⭐ SCORER paths (handleJoinCode / handleSubmitScores) are
 *               DELIBERATELY unchanged — locked-last is correct for a scorer.
 *            2. B-79: rounds.reset_epoch (NEW COLUMN — needs the one-line
 *               migration in _9\migration-b79-reset-epoch-v2.90.0.sql). Every
 *               organizer Reset ALL bumps it; a client that sends reset_epoch
 *               with a score post is refused (409) when its epoch is stale.
 *               Clients that don't send it — every client today — are accepted
 *               unchanged. EVERY new read/write is individually guarded, so a
 *               paste BEFORE the migration behaves exactly like v2.89.0.
 *            3. LATCH: match_play / best_ball stay renderable + PATCHable, but
 *               NEW stamps are refused at all four write sites. "Remove the
 *               OFFER, never the RENDERING" — now enforced server-side.
 *
 *  v2.89.0 — ⭐⭐ FR-01: THE EXTRAORDINARY SCORE ("E.R.") RATER (session 91).
 *            Bruce, s90: flag a round rarer than 1:100 with a unique mark.
 *            NEW: erRate() in arm A of recomputeRoundLeaderboard. The rating
 *            rides INSIDE the existing data JSON as `er` — NO DDL, no new
 *            column, no migration.
 *
 *              Score Differential     = (113 / Slope) x (Adjusted Gross - Rating - PCC)
 *              net Score Differential = Score Differential - Handicap Index
 *
 *            Odds come from the USGA Score Frequency/Probability Table.
 *            ⭐⭐ VERIFIED, not just transcribed: all 16 rows x 9 Handicap Index
 *            bands were read back off the USGA source in s91 and compared cell
 *            by cell against Bruce's screenshot. Every cell matched. The gate
 *            (_15-Preflight/er-verify-s91.mjs) holds an INDEPENDENT second copy
 *            so a one-digit drift fails the build.
 *
 *            ⭐⭐ "Rarer than 1:100" is NOT one net differential. A scratch
 *            player needs -4; a 36 index needs -10. Anything that hardcodes a
 *            single threshold is wrong, and wrong in the direction that
 *            flatters high handicaps. Derived from the band, every time.
 *
 *            ⭐⭐ IT REFUSES RATHER THAN GUESSES. `er` is null — never a
 *            plausible wrong number — for: no Handicap Index (⭐ Number(null)
 *            is 0 and 0 is a REAL band, so an absent index would have been
 *            silently rated as a scratch; guarded explicitly) · an index above
 *            39.9 (USGA: insufficient data) · a NEUTRAL tee, because v2.88.0's
 *            slope 113 / rating = par were invented to avoid inventing
 *            difficulty and a differential off invented numbers is a wrong
 *            rarity claim · fewer than 18 holes · no honest par · a Callaway
 *            event, gated on the ROUND and the EVENT format (Bruce: "we don't
 *            need E.R. for callaway events, does not apply") — Callaway exists
 *            precisely because nobody has an index.
 *
 *            Adjusted Gross uses the WHS net double bogey cap off the COURSE
 *            handicap (a scoring-record rule, not a competition rule). PCC does
 *            not exist in this app: it is 0 and it is REPORTED as 0, not
 *            silently omitted. Fractional net differentials round by Math.ceil
 *            — the least impressive whole row the score actually achieves — so
 *            the rater can only ever UNDER-claim.
 *
 *            Arm B (scramble team cards) is NOT rated: no index per card.
 *            The round SELECT now also carries e.format AS event_format, the
 *            only other change to existing code.
 *            ⭐ NOTHING ELSE MOVED. v2.88.0's tee_source / rating / slope
 *            contract on both arms is byte-identical.
 *
 *  v2.88.0 — ⭐⭐ B-70: THE HARDCODED CLUB RATINGS ARE GONE (session 90).
 *            Bruce: "remove any hardcoded references to the derrick club
 *            ratings." DELETED: the baked-in map of seven courses, and the
 *            single fallback tee it used for everything else — which carried
 *            the Derrick's Deck 2 rating, slope and par. Every course OUTSIDE
 *            those seven silently played as the Derrick on the EVENT path.
 *            Bruce's
 *            Tower Ranch round computed 69.6/119/par 70 where D1 carries
 *            72.1/128/par 72, so every player was 1-2 strokes short and the
 *            stableford points were wrong with them — 270 of 1,335 live score
 *            rows, across Tower Ranch and Okanagan Bear. ⭐⭐ Nothing looked
 *            broken: the default IS a real club's rating, so it reads as
 *            plausible on every screen. A missing number gets reported; a
 *            wrong one does not.
 *            Rating/slope/par now come from D1 course_tees — 131 courses and
 *            801 tees, versus the seven that were compiled in. ⭐ The worker's
 *            own TRIP path has read course_tees since v2.35; only the EVENT
 *            path was hardcoded. The two now agree.
 *            ⭐⭐ WHEN D1 HAS NO ROW we do NOT substitute another club's
 *            numbers. resolveTee falls back to the WHS NEUTRAL tee — slope 113
 *            (the definition of standard difficulty) with rating = par, which
 *            hands a player exactly their Handicap Index in strokes. It
 *            invents no course difficulty, and `tee_source` reports
 *            'course_tee' | 'neutral' so nothing downstream can read a
 *            fallback as an official rating. Same principle as par_source.
 *            Reads are memoised per recompute, so a 10-group event is one
 *            query, not ten. Fail-soft throughout. No DDL.
 *            ⭐ AFTER PASTING: nets and points on Tower Ranch / Okanagan Bear
 *            rounds MOVE 1-2 strokes once recomputed. That is the fix working.
 *  v2.87.0 — ⭐ THE CALLAWAY DOUBLE-PAR CAP GETS THE SAME PAR FALLBACK
 *            (session 90, Aug 4 2026). ONE change, no new machinery.
 *            computeCallawayStandings() called eventHolePars(cfg) and nothing
 *            else, so on an event with no per-hole pars in its config the
 *            Callaway rule "a hole counts at most DOUBLE PAR toward the
 *            deduction" silently never applied — a blow-up hole counted in
 *            FULL. ⭐⭐ That is the difference from v2.86.0's null points:
 *            here the blind spot produced a WRONG allowance, not a missing
 *            one, and nothing on the board said so. It now reuses the same
 *            courseHolePars() helper v2.86.0 added: event config -> the
 *            event's own tee (course_tees.pars) -> the course (courses.par).
 *            ⭐ Event config still WINS. The v2.66.0 rule is UNTOUCHED — we
 *            still refuse to invent par 4, and par_source still reports
 *            'none' when nothing honest answers; the standings row's
 *            par_source now carries 'course_tee' / 'course' too, exactly as
 *            the round board's already does. Fail-soft: any error is "no
 *            pars", the state this function already handled. Additive, no
 *            DDL, no client change required (nothing reads par_source yet).
 *  v2.86.0 — ⭐ THE PAR FALLBACK + two payload adds (session 89, Aug 4 2026).
 *            1. recomputeRoundLeaderboard's per-hole pars now fall back from
 *               the EVENT CONFIG to D1's own course data — course_tees.pars for
 *               the round's tee, then courses.par. Found s87 on Bruce's event
 *               95: `points` was NULL for every player of an
 *               individual_stableford round because eventHolePars(cfg) reads
 *               ONLY the event config, which that event has never carried. This
 *               was never a lite bug — no stableford round on a config-less
 *               event has ever had server-side points, in the full app either.
 *               ⭐ Event config still WINS (an organizer who set pars meant
 *               them). par_source now reports which source answered:
 *               'event_config' | 'course_tee' | 'course' | 'none'. The v2.66.0
 *               rule is UNTOUCHED — null pars are still a real answer and we
 *               still never invent par 4; we only widened where we look.
 *               SI rides the same precedence so points and stroke allocation
 *               can never come from two different rows.
 *            2. D69 — /api/join-round/:code names the course (course_name off
 *               the EFFECTIVE course id, round's over event's), saving every
 *               lite phone a second request on card open.
 *            3. /api/events/watch round object now carries `format`, so a
 *               watcher can tell an individual round from a scramble.
 *            All three are ADDITIVE and fail-soft. No DDL. No client is
 *            required to change; nothing existing changes shape.
 *  v2.85.0 — B-64 (Bruce s82): tickers must speak the round's language —

 *            now a LAST RESORT, honoured only when per-hole pars are missing
 *            (recompute impossible). Root cause was client-side: the engine
 *            rebuild reset playHdcp to 0 (fixed client v406.0), so phones
 *            posted gross pts that the v2.39.0 trustStored rule then locked
 *            in whenever the tee sheet carried a real playing_handicap.
 *            SAFETY: audited s81 against every existing league round —

 *  v2.77.0 — B-17 + D21 SCORE-COUNTS (session 59).
 *            ⭐ SHIPS WITH A D1 MIGRATION — THE TWO MUST GO TOGETHER. The passkeys
 *            table is rebuilt: UNIQUE moves from account_id to credential_id, so
 *            an account can hold one passkey PER DEVICE (B-17: device B's "Save
 *            Face ID" used to silently steal device A's credential via
 *            ON CONFLICT(account_id) DO UPDATE). handlePasskeyRegister now upserts
 *            keyed on credential_id — re-registering the same device is idempotent,
 *            a new device adds a row. handlePasskeyLogin is unchanged (it already
 *            matches account_id AND credential_id, which works with N rows).
 *            ORDERING: after the rebuild the OLD worker's ON CONFLICT(account_id)
 *            errors; before it, THIS worker's ON CONFLICT(credential_id) errors.
 *            Either way only passkey REGISTRATION is exposed, login never — run
 *            the migration, then paste this worker immediately.
 *            D21: the field_players CASE in handleGetRounds now tests
 *            r.format IN (the three individual formats) instead of
 *            = 'individual_stableford', so individual_low_net / individual_low_gross
 *            rounds get a real field count instead of falling to the groups badge.
 *
 *  v2.76.0 — Q4b: PER-ROUND STANDINGS, ADDITIVE ONLY (session 54).
 *            A NEW table round_leaderboard_cache (round_id, entry_key) holds one
 *            standings row per player (individual rounds, arm A) or per team card
 *            (scramble rounds, arm B) per round. leaderboard_cache, its PRIMARY
 *            KEY (event_id), and recomputeLeaderboard are UNTOUCHED — the event-
 *            wide cache keeps working byte-identically for every existing event.
 *
 *            recomputeRoundLeaderboard(env, roundId) reads scores WHERE round_id
 *            = ? AND gross_score > 0, resolves the ROUND'S own course/tee (falling
 *            back to the event's, same precedence handleFinalizeHandicaps uses),
 *            and REPLACEs the round's rows inside one DB.batch. It is fail-soft
 *            end to end: if the table has not been created yet (the DDL ships
 *            separately and is NOT applied by this worker), it logs and returns —
 *            score submission can never be broken by it.
 *
 *            Called AFTER the existing recomputeLeaderboard calls in BOTH score
 *            write paths (handleSubmitScores via the v2.73.0 resolvedRoundId,
 *            handleSubmitRoundScores via ttg.round_id). Neither existing call
 *            moved or changed.
 *
 *            NEW ROUTE: GET /api/events/:id/rounds/:rid/leaderboard — public
 *            read, exactly like the neighbouring .../rounds/:rid/scores (which
 *            has no auth gate). ?recompute=1 forces a recompute first and THAT
 *            is organizer-Bearer-only (resolveSession + organizer_id check).
 *
 *            Stableford points: arm A reuses leagueHolePts + hcpStrokesForHole +
 *            calcCourseHandicapFull — the ONE proven server-side stableford —
 *            with pars/SI from eventHolePars/eventHoleSI (event config). When the
 *            event config carries no per-hole pars (true of events 49 and 58
 *            today, measured), points is NULL and data.par_source says 'none'.
 *            Arm B points stay NULL on purpose: no server-side team-stableford
 *            math exists anywhere in this worker to reuse, and inventing one
 *            here would not be additive. Decide it with Bruce first.
 *
 *  v2.75.0 — THE GUARDS AND THE BADGE LEARN THE ROUND DIMENSION (session 51).
 *            v2.73.0 added scores.round_id, v2.74.0 became its first reader. These
 *            were the last four readers still keying off the polymorphic group_id.
 *
 *            ⭐ THE s49 HANDOFF CALLED THIS AN UNDER-COUNT. IT IS NOT — MEASURED
 *            AGAINST LIVE D1 AND REFUTED (session 51). The v2.72.0 guards fell back
 *            to an EVENT-WIDE count on team formats, which is a strict SUPERSET of
 *            the round's own scores. They could never permit an edit they should
 *            refuse. The real live defect is the opposite: OVER-BLOCKING. Event 49
 *            round 21 (scramble, 18 scores of its own) was quoted 90 — the whole
 *            tournament — and a team round with ZERO scores of its own was flatly
 *            un-editable whenever any SIBLING round had been scored.
 *
 *            ⭐ THE COUNT IS NOW ONE QUERY, NOT TWO BRANCHES, and it keeps the
 *            legacy leg: round_id = ? OR group_id IN (SELECT id FROM tee_times
 *            WHERE round_id = ?). Same OR shape the v2.73.0 DELETE already uses.
 *            Keeping the legacy leg is what makes this NEVER LESS STRICT on an
 *            individual round. Measured before shipping — the new count returns
 *            72, 0, 18, 0, 288, 270 across every round that has scores, which is
 *            byte-identical to today on all five individual rounds and 90 -> 18
 *            on round 21 (still blocks, now with the honest number).
 *
 *            ⭐⭐ R1 — A STRICTNESS REDUCTION, AND IT IS DELIBERATE. A team round
 *            with no scores of its own now ALLOWS the edits it used to refuse.
 *            Safe because zero scores rows with a NULL round_id belong to an event
 *            that has any rounds (996 NULLs, all in roundless events 66/74/75 —
 *            measured s51). If a pre-v2.73.0 team round ever lands in a
 *            round-bearing event, the OR leg will not cover it. Re-measure first.
 *
 *            ⭐ handleListRounds' two SCORE counters lose their format gate too.
 *            field_players stays gated ON PURPOSE — team slots carry group_id with
 *            roster_id NULL, so ungating it would render "1 of 0 scoring".
 *
 *            ⭐ handleSubmitScores' upsert now COALESCEs round_id, so a re-save
 *            that resolves no round can no longer wipe a good one. Its sibling in
 *            handleSubmitRoundScores is deliberately NOT touched: that path binds
 *            ttg.round_id, which is projected from tt.round_id and used to look up
 *            the round — a NULL there 404s long before the INSERT.
 *
 *  v2.74.0 — THE ROUND REPORT READS round_id (session 50).
 *            handleGetRoundScores now keys off scores.round_id instead of
 *            joining through tee_times ON tt.id = s.group_id. That join assumed
 *            scores.group_id is always a tee_times.id, which holds only for the
 *            tee-sheet write path — handleSubmitScores writes a groups.id — so
 *            every team/scramble card was STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE to
 *            GET /api/events/:eventId/rounds/:roundId/scores. The score saved,
 *            the card said saved, and the round report never showed it.
 *
 *            ⭐ READ-ONLY CHANGE. One query in one handler. No schema, no write
 *            path, no other reader touched. The bind order moves with it —
 *            (eventId, roundId, eventId) becomes (eventId, eventId, roundId) —
 *            because the round parameter leaves the JOIN and becomes the WHERE.
 *
 *            ⭐ v2.73.0's column is what makes this possible and its NULL rule
 *            still holds: 964 legacy rows carry NULL round_id and stay out of
 *            every round report, exactly as they already were. Measured against
 *            live D1 before shipping: of the 630 rows the old join could see,
 *            all 630 have s.round_id = tt.round_id — so no row moves and none
 *            is lost; 18 rows become visible that never were.
 *
 *  v2.73.0 — THE ROUND DIMENSION ON SCORES (session 48).
 *            scores gains a round_id INTEGER REFERENCES rounds(id) column, with
 *            index idx_scores_round_id. Both write paths now STAMP it:
 *            handleSubmitRoundScores already holds ttg.round_id and binds it
 *            straight into the INSERT; handleSubmitScores has no round in hand
 *            — groups carries no round_id — so it RESOLVES one through
 *            tee_time_players → tee_times → rounds, scoped to the group's own
 *            event, and binds null when the group is in no tee sheet at all.
 *            handleDeleteRound now deletes by round_id, keeping the legacy
 *            group_id-in-tee_times clause beside it so nothing is missed.
 *
 *            ⭐ THE COLUMN IS BACKFILLED, NOT COMPLETE. 558 rows were attributed
 *            to a round; 964 stay NULL — three legacy events that have no rounds
 *            at all, so there is no round to attribute them to and never was.
 *            Every reader must treat NULL as "not known", never as "no round".
 *
 *            ⭐ ADDITIVE ONLY. UNIQUE(player_id, hole, group_id) is UNCHANGED, so
 *            the conflict target and the upsert semantics are exactly what they
 *            were. round_id joins the DO UPDATE SET so a re-post corrects a row
 *            stamped before this shipped. No existing read path is touched.
 *
 *  v2.72.0 — EVENT & ROUND FIELD LOCKING (session 47, banner backdated).
 *            The guards shipped in session 47 and have been in this file since,
 *            but the banner was never bumped — recorded here now so the version
 *            history is honest.
 *
 *            course_id, tee and format are REFUSED WITH 409 once any score is
 *            posted, on BOTH handleUpdateEvent and handleUpdateRound. They feed
 *            the course-handicap maths, so PATCHing one after cards are in
 *            silently re-computes results already entered. name, date, rules,
 *            config, status and course_name stay editable on purpose — none of
 *            them enter the net-score arithmetic and the organizer needs them
 *            on the day. status above all: locking it would strand a live event.
 *
 *            handleDeleteRound refuses a round with posted scores by the same
 *            rule. On team formats the count falls back to the EVENT-wide total
 *            and says so — over-blocking, which is the correct way to be wrong
 *            about a posted score.
 *
 *  v2.70.0 — GAME-DAY DATES ON THE LEAGUES LIST (Bruce, session 30).
 *            handleListLeagues now returns first_round_date and last_round_date
 *            — MIN and MAX of league_rounds.round_date — alongside the existing
 *            member_count / round_count subqueries.
 *
 *            WHY: a league row has NO date of its own. The table carries only
 *            created_at, so the leagues screen could only ever be ordered by
 *            when a league was CREATED. Bruce asked for what is current or
 *            soonest to sit at the top, and a league's real date is its season.
 *
 *            ⭐ DELIBERATELY NO DATE MATH HERE. The obvious version of this
 *            computes "the next game day" with date('now'), but date('now') is
 *            UTC — in MDT that flips six hours early every evening and would
 *            silently reorder the screen at 6pm. MIN/MAX are plain aggregates
 *            with no clock in them; client v329.0 owns the today-vs-past call
 *            and does it against the DEVICE's local date.
 *
 *            ⭐ ORDER BY l.created_at DESC IS KEPT ON PURPOSE. The client sorts
 *            now, so this is no longer the user-visible order — but leaving it
 *            gives a deterministic base order for leagues whose seasons tie,
 *            and it keeps older clients behaving exactly as they do today.
 *
 *            ⭐ ADDITIVE ONLY. Two new columns on one read endpoint. No schema
 *            change, no write path touched, nothing removed. A client that has
 *            never heard of these fields is unaffected.

 *  v2.69.0 — MULTI-GAME ROUNDS, PHASE 1 (Bruce, session 18 — BIND).
 *            Spec: claude/multi-game-rounds-spec-v1.md §1-§4 plus
 *                  claude/multi-game-spec-addendum-s18.md §1-§5.
 *
 *            THE BUG THIS CLOSES: low_net / low_gross / match_play / best_ball
 *            were selectable in three client pickers and whitelisted here at
 *            LEAGUE_FORMATS, and NONE of them had a scoring branch anywhere.
 *            Pick Low Net and you got a round labelled "Low Net" that scored
 *            as nothing, silently, with no error. Only stableford and
 *            overall_skins ever computed anything.
 *
 *            LOW NET and LOW GROSS now score. The numbers already existed —
 *            v2.65.0 shipped total_net / hole_net as deliberately additive
 *            fields. This ranks on them. Match Play and Best Ball still do
 *            not score and are being REMOVED from the client pickers in the
 *            same release; they stay in LEAGUE_FORMATS so a round already
 *            stamped with one keeps rendering. Remove the OFFER, never the
 *            RENDERING.
 *
 *            trip_rounds.games TEXT (ALTER run live and verified in s17) is
 *            now read and written. It is an OBJECT, merged ONE level by the
 *            existing _cfgMerge — an array cannot merge, and "merge, never
 *            replace" is the v2.68.1 hazard one depth down.
 *              { "list": ["stableford","low_net"], "season": "stableford" }
 *            format stays AUTHORITATIVE as the primary game. games.list is
 *            every game on the round and must contain format. games.season
 *            names which game feeds the season points column.
 *
 *            §1 BIND IS ENFORCED AT WRITE TIME, NOT ASSUMED. games.season may
 *            only name a HIGH-WINS game (stableford, overall_skins). Naming a
 *            low-wins one is a 400. So the organizer picks — Bruce, s18 — but
 *            can never pick a unit that would rank the worst golfer first.
 *            Net strokes are ~72 where high is good; skins are ~2.
 *            games.season = null means the week accrues NOTHING: no points,
 *            no weeks_played, no win. It posts its boards and stops.
 *
 *            DEFAULT IS EXACTLY TODAY'S BEHAVIOUR, by construction:
 *              seasonGame = ('season' in games) ? games.season
 *                         : (HIGH_WINS.has(format) ? format : 'stableford')
 *            All 8 existing rounds have games NULL, so a stableford week feeds
 *            stableford, a skins week feeds skins (v2.58.0, unchanged), and a
 *            low_net week feeds stableford — which is precisely what it
 *            silently does today, the format having had no engine at all.
 *
 *            ALSO CLOSED: handleUpdateTripRound wrote `format` with NO
 *            whitelist check. The league PATCH has always checked
 *            LEAGUE_FORMATS; the trip-side round PATCH never has, so it
 *            stored any string handed to it. Same silent-acceptance family as
 *            the finding above. New writes are gated; existing rows are
 *            untouched so nothing already stored stops rendering.
 *
 *            INCOMPLETE ROUNDS SORT BELOW COMPLETE ONES on a low board — most
 *            holes played first, then lowest strokes. Ranking a 9-hole 36 net
 *            ahead of an 18-hole 70 would be obviously wrong, and total-stroke
 *            games are the only ones where holes played changes the unit.
 *            Stableford and skins are additive and do not have this problem.
 *
 *            PURELY ADDITIVE otherwise: every pre-existing field, sort and
 *            rank on every payload is untouched, so an old client renders
 *            exactly what it always did on a stableford or a skins week.

 *  v2.68.3 — RULES CORRECTION (Bruce, session 16 — BIND). 2v2 Best Ball Net
 *            ran at FULL course handicap. IT SHOULD NOT. All three Barnes
 *            games zero off the LOWEST course handicap among the FOUR players
 *            in the match — best ball is not the exception it was built as.
 *            The allocator (barnesPlayHdcps, all four, one subtraction) now
 *            runs BEFORE any branch reads a net, so no game can drift from
 *            the others by editing one branch.
 *            NO WEEK NEEDS RE-SETTLING: no Barnes week has ever been scored.
 *            Week 14, the first, was rained out. This lands before the money
 *            is real.
 *            UNCHANGED: par points / stableford stay at FULL handicap, and
 *            they decide the $20 team pot and both $5 pots. So on a Best Ball
 *            week a player's MATCH net (low-to-zero) and his PAR-POINTS net
 *            (full) are different numbers, exactly as on the other two weeks.
 *            That is deliberate — one rule for matches, one for the money.
 *            Also unchanged: 18 on the table / 12 the ceiling, maxSwing 1,
 *            and the refusal above course handicap 36.

 *  v2.68.2 — ONE COLUMN. GET /api/leagues/:id returned every round's date,
 *            trip and counts but NOT its `config`, so a setup screen could
 *            write barnes_week and never read it back — every save would
 *            have been a blind overwrite of a value it could not show.

 *            round_config to match handleLeagueBarnes' existing alias
 *            rather than inventing a second name for the same column.

 *  v2.68.1 — DEEP MERGE FOR THE TWO CONFIG SUB-OBJECTS. v2.68.0 merged only
 *            at the TOP level, so `{barnes:{on:true}}` REPLACED the whole
 *            barnes object — dropping teams, stake and carry_in — while
 *            game_pars and game_si survived untouched beside it. The smoke
 *            test named in the s14 intro checks exactly those survivors, so
 *            it would have reported PASS on the very write that emptied the
 *            league. `{barnes_week:{game:'2v2_highlow'}}` had the same shape
 *            of bug against `lineup`. Nobody hit either: no caller existed
 *            yet, which is the only reason this is a fix and not an incident.
 *            `_cfgMerge` now takes a `deepKeys` list — a named key merges ONE
 *            level deeper when both sides are plain objects, an explicit null
 *            still deletes a sub-key, and every other key keeps the old
 *            replace semantics. Both handlers now re-validate the MERGED
 *            result, not just the incoming patch, because a patch that is
 *            legal alone can compose into an illegal whole.
 *            Harness: repro-league-config.mjs — 45/45, of which 16 flip
 *            against the pre-fix file. Its §8 mutation self-test re-runs
 *            the survival assertions against v2.68.0's own merge; if any
 *            of them passes there it is vacuous. Do not delete §8.

 *  v2.68.0 — LEAGUE CONFIG WRITERS. v2.67.0 read leagues.config and
 *            league_rounds.config; nothing wrote either, so Barnes could only
 *            be configured by direct D1 write. Two new routes, both additive:
 *              PATCH /api/leagues/:id/config              { barnes, game_pars, game_si }
 *              PATCH /api/leagues/:id/rounds/:rid/config  { barnes_week }
 *            SHALLOW MERGE at the top level, not replace. leagues.config holds
 *            barnes + game_pars + game_si together and handleLeagueBarnes
 *            REFUSES without the latter two, so a setup screen that only knows
 *            about barnes must not wipe them by omission. Explicit null deletes
 *            a key; absence never does. Same read-modify-write trap the v2.58.0
 *            skins note warns about, one table over.
 *            VALIDATES AT WRITE TIME. barnes_week.game is checked against
 *            BARNES_GAMES and a NUMBER is rejected by name — session 13 nearly
 *            stored "game": 1 against a table keyed '1v1_nassau', which would
 *            have surfaced days later as no_game_set and read as organizer
 *            error. Lineups are cross-checked against the team rosters, so a
 *            player cannot be entered for a team he is not on.
 *            Separate routes rather than a `config` key on handlePatchLeague:
 *            that handler hard-requires `name` because it is the rename path.
 *            ALSO FIXED: /api/ping reported 2.64.0 — three versions stale,
 *            despite the v2.36.0 note claiming it tracks the real version.
 *  v2.67.0 — BARNES WEEK & YEAR LONG. New league format: 3 teams x 6, one of
 *            three match games chosen weekly, all normalised to 18 points on
 *            the table and 12 the team ceiling. Additive — one new route and
 *            one new block of functions; nothing existing is touched.
 *            TWO OF THE THREE GAMES ARE THE CLIENT'S EXISTING NASSAU ENGINE,
 *            ported verbatim (holeResult's 1v1 branch, and its 2v2-combined
 *            HIGH-LOW branch — which is NOT aggregate net). Only game 3, 2v2
 *            best ball net, is new code. (v2.68.3: it ran at FULL handicap
 *            and no longer does — all three zero off the low man.) Presses are OFF,
 *            so the client's press chain is not ported rather than ported and
 *            disabled.
 *            STROKES: uses this worker's existing hcpStrokesForHole, never a
 *            second allocator. Checked exhaustively over si 1..18 x hcp 0..60:
 *            identical to the client's matchNetS for every hcp 0..36, first
 *            diverging at 37. So the board REFUSES above 36 instead of quietly
 *            disagreeing with the in-round match board. League 10's highest
 *            full course handicap at Derrick Deck 2 is 23.
 *            REFUSES rather than guesses on: no game set for the week (it never
 *            defaults to game 1 — null is a real answer), missing per-hole
 *            pars/SI, missing lineup, missing tee rating.
 *            The two $5 pots RE-DERIVE stableford from gross through
 *            leagueHolePts; stored stableford_pts is not trusted (the Quota
 *            lesson: a stored points column held a different game's number).
 *            scoreRange's early-settle test is corrected to holesLeft*maxSwing
 *            because High-Low swings 2 a hole. It changes no awarded point
 *            (those settle at holesLeft===0) — only the live board.
 *  v2.66.0 — Wave-1 games part 1: Quota — GET /api/events/:id/games?game=quota, additive, July 28 2026
 * Previous: 2.65.0 (Low gross / low net — additive net totals on league round, league season, and trip leaderboards, July 28 2026)
 *
 *  NOTE ON THIS HEADER: it read "Version: 2.45.0" all the way through v2.49.0,
 *  so grepping the file's own banner told you nothing about what it was. Keep it
 *  current — it is the first thing anyone looks at.
 *
 *  v2.66.0 — WAVE-1 GAMES, PART 1: QUOTA (Bruce, July 28 2026 — games spec
 *            claude/games-expansion-spec-v1.md §1.1, decisions this session).
 *            GET /api/events/:id/games?game=quota — organizer-auth, computed
 *            fresh, never cached. ADDITIVE: no schema change, no new table,
 *            no existing handler modified.
 *
 *            DECIDED (Bruce, July 28 2026):
 *              - CLASSIC Quota. Points are earned on GROSS vs par; the
 *                handicap enters ONCE, through the target: quota = 36 - CH.
 *                The rejected alternative was net points against a flat 36,
 *                which makes Quota mathematically identical to the MMG
 *                stableford board — a game that shows nothing new. These two
 *                are NOT interchangeable: max(0, 2+diff) floors at a different
 *                place depending on whether the stroke was applied, so a
 *                blow-up hole scores differently in each.
 *              - base = 36, RE-DERIVED and confirmed, not inherited. The spec
 *                carried base:36 from the TORIIE 8/4/2/1/0 scale. Re-derived
 *                against OUR ramp: leagueHolePts is max(0, 2 + diff), so par
 *                is worth 2, and 18 x 2 = 36. Independent of course par — a
 *                par-70 course still totals 36 quota points, because the 2 is
 *                per HOLE, not per stroke. The number survives; the reasoning
 *                behind it was wrong and is now recorded.
 *              - Missing per-hole pars => the game is UNAVAILABLE, with a
 *                reason. It does NOT fall back to par 4 (see below).
 *              - Non-MMG event => UNAVAILABLE, with a reason. The roster path
 *                below is the MMG one; on any other format it returns nothing,
 *                and an empty board reads as "nobody has scored" rather than
 *                "this game does not run here".
 *
 *            NO NEW SCORING TABLE. Gross points come from the existing
 *            leagueHolePts with playingHcp = 0: hcpStrokesForHole(si, 0)
 *            returns 0, so net collapses to gross and the standard ramp
 *            applies unchanged. storedPts is ALWAYS passed null — stored
 *            stableford_pts are NET and would silently poison a gross game.
 *
 *            ⚠ PER-HOLE PARS — the reason this endpoint can refuse.
 *            Events resolve courses through COURSE_DATA (line ~599), which
 *            carries slope/rating/TOTAL par only. Per-hole pars live in D1
 *            course_tees and are reachable only on the trip/league routes.
 *            computeMmgStandings works around this by reading config.mmg_pars
 *            and falling back to par 4 on every hole. That fallback is
 *            survivable for NET stableford; for GROSS Quota it is fatal — on
 *            a course with four par 3s and two par 5s a dead-level-par round
 *            scores 38 instead of 36, and nothing anywhere says so.
 *            Bruce (July 28 2026, BIND): REFUSE, don't guess. The client
 *            writes config.game_pars (ptGetHolePars is already the single
 *            source of truth for per-hole pars and already resolves event
 *            courses); mmg_pars is accepted as a fallback for MMG events.
 *
 *  v2.65.0 — LOW GROSS / LOW NET (Bruce, July 28 2026 — games spec step 1).
 *            Purely ADDITIVE payload fields; no existing field, sort, or
 *            ranking changes, so an old client renders exactly what it did.
 *            (a) League round leaderboard: per-player f9_net / b9_net /
 *            total_net summed from the SAME hole_net array v2.58.0 added
 *            (net = gross − hcpStrokesForHole strokes; holes without a
 *            score contribute nothing, mirroring f9/b9/total_gross).
 *            (b) League season: week entries carry net; season standings
 *            accumulate total_net beside total_gross (raw sums — weeks_played
 *            already tells the reader who missed weeks).
 *            (c) Trip leaderboard: each roundBreakdown entry carries net;
 *            standings carry total_gross + total_net across rounds.
 *            Net sums are gated the way gross is (score > 0 counts), so a
 *            round in progress shows a running net, not a fake 18-hole one.
 *  v2.64.0 — MMG GUEST LINKING (Bruce, July 27 2026). Event 62's import
 *            created scoped duplicates for 51 guests who already exist in the
 *            permanent roster WITH handicaps — the v2.63.0 design matched
 *            members only. Amended (Bruce's call): handleMmgImport's guest
 *            branch now accepts t.guest.roster_id (verified same-account,
 *            unscoped) and LINKS the permanent row instead of creating a
 *            scoped one; response adds linked_guests. Unmatched guests are
 *            scoped exactly as before. Client v285.0 preflights guests through
 *            _ptMatchToRoster same as members. Old clients (no roster_id) get
 *            v2.63.0 behavior unchanged.
 *
 *  v2.63.0 — MMG PHASE 1 (Bruce, July 27 2026 — spec: claude/mmg-spec-v1.md,
 *            all §10 decisions BIND). Member/Guest one-day two-man team event,
 *            net stableford, two-man COMBINED total. 30 sixsomes (3 teams per
 *            start unit 1A/1B…18A/18B), shotgun; cards are three FOURSOMES per
 *            sixsome via a 3-cycle rotation (A keeps C's card → C keeps B's →
 *            B keeps A's). ALL ADDITIVE. D1 migrations — RUN BEFORE PASTING
 *            THIS VERSION (safe with v2.62.0 live; columns are nullable):
 *              ALTER TABLE groups ADD COLUMN scores_for_group_id INTEGER;
 *              ALTER TABLE groups ADD COLUMN start_unit TEXT;
 *              ALTER TABLE roster ADD COLUMN event_scope_id INTEGER;
 *            Pieces:
 *            - events accept format 'mmg' (no validation change needed — the
 *              format column was always free-form).
 *            - groups carry scores_for_group_id (directional: "the group whose
 *              card I keep") + start_unit (organizer-typed label, e.g. '4A').
 *              handleJoinCode pairing: pointer FIRST, same-start-hole
 *              derivation as the fallback — RBC events unchanged (their
 *              pointer is NULL). Fail-soft legacy retry if the columns are
 *              missing (pre-migration paste).
 *            - roster.event_scope_id = event-scoped guest rows (MMG guests).
 *              handleRosterList EXCLUDES scoped rows by default (fail-soft
 *              legacy retry); ?event_scope=<eventId> lists ONLY that event's
 *              guests. handleRosterUpsert accepts event_scope_id and matches
 *              within the same scope only (unscoped path byte-identical).
 *            - POST /api/events/:id/mmg-import {teams:[{member,guest}]} —
 *              bulk pair import: member links by roster_id / exact unscoped
 *              name match / create:true; guest created event-scoped (blank
 *              handicap; Bruce fills in later). Creates the 90 groups with
 *              both players (member sort 0, guest sort 1). Idempotent-ish:
 *              a duplicate team name for the event is skipped.
 *            - computeMmgStandings = THE MMG math (one implementation):
 *              per-player net stableford via leagueHolePts + hcpStrokesForHole
 *              (full course handicap, Math.round(calcCourseHandicap), missing
 *              handicap → 0 strokes), SI/pars from event config mmg_si /
 *              mmg_pars (fallback si=hole order, par 4), team = sum of both
 *              players' points. Tiebreak per config.mmg_tiebreak:
 *              'share' (default) or 'back9' (combined B9 → B6 → B3 → 18th).
 *              Ranks via applyTieAwareRanks.
 *            - GET /api/events/:id/mmg-leaderboard (organizer) computes fresh;
 *              recomputeLeaderboard delegates to the SAME function for
 *              format 'mmg' so leaderboard_cache / handleWatch serve MMG
 *              standings to the watch page. Scramble/RBC caching untouched.
 *            - finalize-handicaps deliberately untouched: MMG has no team
 *              handicap; 2-man groups fall into its existing <4-players skip.
 *
 *  v2.62.0 — REPORT DATA (Bruce, July 28 2026). One ADDITIVE field: each
 *            trip-leaderboard STANDINGS entry now carries handicap (the
 *            player's current GC index, current_index ?? handicap — the same
 *            source every per-round PH derivation already reads) so the
 *            Overall Reports can print "Current Handicap [X.X]" beside the
 *            name. handleLeagueSeason needs nothing: its week entries have
 *            carried the same field since v2.39.0. Old clients ignore it.
 *
 *  v2.61.0 — TRIP REPORT DATA (Bruce, July 28 2026). One ADDITIVE column:
 *            the trip-leaderboard breakdown now carries tee_name (the
 *            PLAYER'S tee for that round — ct was already joined per player)
 *            so the reworked Overall Report can print date · course · tee ·
 *            time per round line. Nothing else changes; old clients ignore it.
 *
 *  v2.60.0 — TRIP TICKER DATA (July 27 2026). Purely ADDITIVE fields on
 *            GET /api/trips/:id/leaderboard for the Trip & League Live
 *            Ticker (client v266.0): each round breakdown now carries
 *            tee_time_id + start_time (from the tee-time join that was
 *            already there), and each entry in `rounds` carries status +
 *            tee_times (distinct tee times WITH players). The ticker shows
 *            only when tee_times >= 2 — Bruce's condition — and sorts the
 *            strip in tee-time order. handleLeagueSeason's twin bulk-query
 *            block is deliberately NOT touched — the season page has no
 *            ticker. Old clients ignore all four fields; no query added,
 *            no existing field changed.
 *
 *  v2.59.0 — LEAGUE RENAME (Bruce, July 27 2026). PATCH /api/leagues/:id
 *            {name, description?}. The name is not cosmetic: every game day's
 *            hidden trip was named leagueDayTripName(league.name, date) at
 *            birth, so the rename REBUILDS those names — or season standings
 *            would carry the old league name forever. Same custom-name rule
 *            the date PATCH uses: only a trip still named exactly what create
 *            wrote is rebuilt; a name the organizer typed is left alone.
 *            ONE batch — the league row and every trip name move together.
 *
 *  v2.58.0 — SKINS (Phase C). "Overall Skins" was selectable and computed
 *            nothing. Now handleLeagueRoundLeaderboard branches on the round
 *            format and handleLeagueSeason feeds the standings.
 *            THE league's rules (Bruce, July 27 2026):
 *              NET   — strokes per hole via hcpStrokesForHole, the SAME
 *                      allocator stableford uses. Never a second one.
 *              CARRY — a tied hole's skin rolls forward and stacks.
 *              DEAD  — a stack still riding after 18 is lost. No walk-back
 *                      to hole 1, no roll to next week.
 *              GUESTS are eligible — they can take a hole and the money.
 *            computeSkins() is THE skins engine; both handlers call it, so a
 *            week's count can never disagree with the season it feeds. On a
 *            skins week hole_pts carries SKINS won per hole, so the existing
 *            sum(hole_pts) === pts invariant still holds and the scorecard
 *            renders unchanged.
 *            Money is organizer-selected PER GAME DAY on two new nullable
 *            trip_rounds columns (skins_mode, skins_amount) — additive, same
 *            shape the format column was. count | pool | fixed. Pool is
 *            buy-in x the field that POSTED SCORES, divided by skins won;
 *            arithmetic in cents and the residue of an uneven split gets its
 *            own line rather than being absorbed into a payout. Zero skins
 *            won all night = pool shown unclaimed; no payout is invented.
 *
 *  v2.57.0 — GAME DAY DATE IS EDITABLE. PATCH /api/leagues/:id/rounds/:rid
 *            now takes round_date as well as format; either key alone is a
 *            valid body, both together are one call. Moving a day is FOUR
 *            rows kept in lockstep, in ONE statement batch:
 *              league_rounds.round_date  (what the schedule reads)
 *              trips.start_date/end_date (the wrapped one-day trip's span)
 *              trip_rounds.date          (round 1 — what the round card shows)
 *            handleCreateLeagueRound writes all four at birth; a PATCH that
 *            moved only one of them would leave the day showing two dates
 *            depending on which screen you were looking at.
 *            The auto-generated trip NAME carries the date too ("Wed League
 *            — Jul 30, 2026") and surfaces in season standings, the round
 *            leaderboard and the scorer's join screen. It is regenerated ONLY
 *            when it still matches what create would have produced for the
 *            OLD date — an organizer's custom trip name is never clobbered.
 *            leagueDayTripName() is THE name builder; create and patch both
 *            call it, so the two can never drift.
 *
 *  v2.56.0 — LEAGUE GAME DAYS (Bruce, July 27). A league week is a trip in
 *            a trenchcoat (league_rounds → trips → trip_rounds), and there was
 *            NO way to delete one — only Delete League. Now:
 *            - DELETE /api/leagues/:id/rounds/:rid — removes one game day:
 *              full trip cascade + the league_rounds link. tripCascade() is
 *              the ONE cascade implementation, shared with handleDeleteTrip
 *              and handleDeleteLeague — never fork a copy.
 *            - PATCH /api/leagues/:id/rounds/:rid {format} — set/change the
 *              week's format after creation ("Format: TBD" used to be
 *              permanent). Validated against the format set; null clears.
 *            - handleGetLeague rounds now carry players_done (players with
 *              all 18 holes in) for the schedule's scored-status pills.
 *            - handleDeleteLeague now cascades each game-day TRIP too — it
 *              used to delete only the link rows, orphaning every trip.
 *  v2.55.0 — POST /api/bets/races/:id/token (FULL organizer session only)
 *            mints a racetok:* credential — 12 h TTL, valid ONLY for that
 *            race, accepted ONLY by the four endpoints the tote calls
 *            (GET race, POST/DELETE wagers, PATCH places) via the shared
 *            betsRaceAccess gate. Nothing else accepts it, and it cannot
 *            mint another. WHY: ptBetsOpenRace put the organizer's full
 *            24-hour session token in the tote URL — address bar, history,
 *            every pasted link was the whole account. Now a leaked tote URL
 *            buys one race's betting for a day, not the account.
 *  v2.54.0 — DELETE /api/bets/races/:id/wagers?ticket_ref=REF (organizer-
 *            authed). Voids ONE ticket: deletes every wager on the race
 *            carrying that ticket_ref. The tote has had the triple-confirm
 *            void ceremony since v20.x but only edited LOCAL state — D1 (the
 *            system of record) kept the slip, so the only honest remedy for
 *            a mis-entered slip was deleting the whole race. 409 if the race
 *            is settled (payouts are final — void before settle or delete
 *            the race); 404 if the ticket_ref has no wagers on this race.
 *            Returns { ok, ticket_ref, deleted_count, voided_total }.
 *  v2.53.0 — POST /api/{events|trips|leagues}/:id/duplicate (organizer-
 *            authed). People + settings only into a fresh DRAFT with fresh
 *            codes: event copies config verbatim (event_roster/scoring/

 *            ineligible_reason); trip copies trip_players; league copies

 *            tee shots, leaderboard_cache, or bets races.
 *  v2.52.0 — handleJoinCode's group payload now includes the event's public
 *            event_code (SELECT already joined events; one extra column).
 *            PURELY ADDITIVE — old clients ignore it. Lets the scoring
 *            client poll the no-auth POST /api/events/watch for the scramble
 *            live ticker without any new endpoint or auth surface.
 *  v2.51.0 — handleJoinRound rejects codes for non-active events with 409
 *            {closed:true} BEFORE handing out a card. Found live by Bruce:
 *            joining a foursome code on an ARCHIVED event returned a fully
 *            scored card, then handleSubmitRoundScores 409'd every save
 *            ("Event is not active") — the client retried forever, one red
 *            "Score send failed" toast every 30-45s, no way out. Same shape
 *            as the v2.46.0 lock check and the live client already renders
 *            a non-404 join error cleanly, so old clients degrade politely.
 *            'complete'/'archived' say the event has ended; anything else
 *            non-active says scoring is not open yet. Submit-side gates are
 *            untouched (they were never the bug). Pairs with client v250.2,
 *            which additionally halts its retry loop on ANY 409 and shows
 *            one persistent banner instead of endless toasts.
 *  v2.50.0 — handleGetBetsRace additionally returns a top-level `round` key
 *            (NULL for an overall race): id, round_number, format, date,
 *            session, looked up by primary key from `rounds` via race.round_id.
 *            PURELY ADDITIVE — `race` itself is untouched, so a pre-v2.50.0
 *            tote ignores it and behaves exactly as before.
 *            WHY: the tote minted every ticket as LCI-<year>-0001 from a
 *            per-race counter, so all nine races on event 49 issued the same
 *            ref; and its betting sheet hardcoded "Texas Scramble" plus a
 *            "TEAM NAME" column over the 40 individual players. runner_mode /

 *            runner shape was always available — what it could NOT derive was
 *            the round NUMBER (round_id 22 is not "R4") or the round's format.
 *            Both live on `rounds`, so one indexed lookup supplies the ticket
 *            prefix (R4G-) and an honest format line in the same trip.
 *            Pairs with tote Pards-Bets v20.10. Deploy the worker FIRST: on
 *            v2.49.0 the tote gets no `round`, so every round race would mint
 *            OVP-/OVG- refs and drop the round number from the sheet header.
 *  v2.45.0 — handleSubmitRoundScores rejects foursome-code submits when
 *            rounds.status = 'locked' (409). The lock is toggled by the
 *            organizer via the existing PATCH /api/rounds/:id {status} —
 *            no new endpoint. Closes the tamper window: printed foursome
 *            codes stayed live until the EVENT was marked complete, so any
 *            golfer with a tee sheet could rescore a played round days
 *            later. Correction flow: Unlock → code → fix → Relock.
 *            Rounds default 'active' (unlocked) — behavior unchanged until
 *            the organizer locks. Additive — old clients unaffected.

 *  v2.43.0 — SCALE CORRECTION (Bruce, July 26 2026). The tour Stableford
 *            scale is now the standard ramp: max(0, 2 + diff), i.e.
 *            4 under 6 / 3 under 5 / 2 under 4 / 1 under 3 / fixed 2 /
 *            1 over 1 / 2+ over or no score 0. This LOWERS eagle (5->4)
 *            and albatross (6->5); birdie and below are unchanged.
 *            The v2.40.0 hole-in-one=8 override is DROPPED at all sites
 *            — an ace now scores by diff (4 on a par 3, 5 on a par 4,
 *            6 on a par 5). Client v247.4 carries the identical helper;
 *            the two MUST ship together or leaderboard and live entry
 *            drift apart again (the v2.39.0/v2.42.0 class of bug).
 *            NOTE: league stored stableford_pts written under an older
 *            scale are still trusted when playing_handicap is non-NULL
 *            (leaguePlayingHcp/trustStored) — history is NOT rewritten.
 *  v2.42.0 — TRIP twin of the v2.39.0 league fix (found by Bruce on the
 *            Scottsdale trip, Round 3): trip_tee_time_players rows created
 *            before v2.39.0 have playing_handicap = NULL, and
 *            handleTripLeaderboard did `?? 0` — whole foursomes scored with
 *            ZERO strokes (gross stableford, not net). Now: when
 *            playing_handicap is NULL, DERIVE it server-side with
 *            calcCourseHandicapFull(current_index, slope, rating, par) using
 *            the player's own tee when set, else the round tee. Payload gains
 *            `hcp_derived` per round, mirroring the league fix. Rows with a
 *            REAL playing_handicap (incl. legitimate 0) are trusted untouched.
 *  v2.41.0 — NEW code-authenticated endpoint:

 *            Body: { foursome_code, players:[{trip_player_id, handicap}] }
 *            Auth = foursome code + round match ONLY (same trust level as

 *            trip_player_id in the body MUST already sit in that code's

 *            The CLIENT fetches the indexes (stored per-device Golf Canada

 *   maybeAutoSettleBetsRace fires on every tee-time score submit. Before this
 *   version it selected EVERY open race_type='round' race on the event, read
 *   the whole-event GROUP leaderboard_cache, and name-matched the top 3 into
 *   bets_places. That was survivable while an event had exactly one race. On
 *   event 49 it is not: five of the nine races are team races carrying those
 *   same 10 group names, so the first completed round would have silently
 *   written identical placements into all five — including two rounds not yet
 *   played and the overall. On live money. The flag makes manual placement the
 *   default and auto-settlement a deliberate act. Nothing sets it to 1 yet.

 *   );

 *     id         INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
 *     race_id    INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES bets_races(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,

 *     UNIQUE(race_id, position)

 * Changes from 2.28.3:
 *   - GET /api/trips/by-viewer-pin/:pin — public PIN lookup

const ALLOWED_ORIGINS = [
  'https://bdcomeau.github.io',
  'https://pardstour.com',        // v2.83.0 — custom domain (Cloudflare-registered, GitHub Pages)
  'https://www.pardstour.com',    // v2.83.0 — www variant
  'http://localhost:3000',
  'http://localhost:8080',
];

function corsHeaders(origin) {
  const allowed = ALLOWED_ORIGINS.includes(origin) ? origin : ALLOWED_ORIGINS[0];
  return {
    'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': allowed,
    'Access-Control-Allow-Methods': 'GET, POST, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS',
    'Access-Control-Allow-Headers': 'Content-Type, Authorization, X-Team-Pin',
    'Access-Control-Max-Age': '86400',
  };
}

function json(data, status = 200, origin = '') {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), {
    status,
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', ...corsHeaders(origin) },
  });
}

function err(msg, status = 400, origin = '', extra = null) {
  // v2.46.0 — `extra` lets an error carry a machine-readable flag alongside the
  // human message (e.g. {locked:true}) so the client can style it without
  // string-matching the copy. Omitted everywhere else; shape is unchanged.
  return json(extra ? { error: msg, ...extra } : { error: msg }, status, origin);
}

const PBKDF2_ITERATIONS = 100_000;

function bufToHex(buf) {
  return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('');
}

function hexToBuf(hex) {
  const bytes = new Uint8Array(hex.length / 2);
  for (let i = 0; i < hex.length; i += 2) bytes[i / 2] = parseInt(hex.slice(i, i + 2), 16);
  return bytes.buffer;
}

async function hashPassword(password) {
  const salt = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(16));
  const key = await crypto.subtle.importKey('raw', new TextEncoder().encode(password), 'PBKDF2', false, ['deriveBits']);
  const derived = await crypto.subtle.deriveBits({ name: 'PBKDF2', salt, iterations: PBKDF2_ITERATIONS, hash: 'SHA-256' }, key, 256);
  return `${bufToHex(salt.buffer)}:${PBKDF2_ITERATIONS}:${bufToHex(derived)}`;
}

async function verifyPassword(password, stored) {
  const [saltHex, iterStr, hashHex] = stored.split(':');
  const salt = hexToBuf(saltHex);
  const key = await crypto.subtle.importKey('raw', new TextEncoder().encode(password), 'PBKDF2', false, ['deriveBits']);
  const derived = await crypto.subtle.deriveBits({ name: 'PBKDF2', salt: new Uint8Array(salt), iterations: parseInt(iterStr, 10), hash: 'SHA-256' }, key, 256);
  return bufToHex(derived) === hashHex;
}

const SESSION_TTL_SECONDS = 86_400;

function generateToken() {
  return bufToHex(crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32)).buffer);
}

async function createSession(env, accountId) {
  const token = generateToken();
  await env.SESSIONS.put(`session:${token}`, String(accountId), { expirationTtl: SESSION_TTL_SECONDS });
  return token;
}

async function resolveSession(env, req) {
  const auth = req.headers.get('Authorization') || '';
  const token = auth.startsWith('Bearer ') ? auth.slice(7).trim() : null;
  if (!token) return null;
  const accountId = await env.SESSIONS.get(`session:${token}`);
  if (!accountId) return null;
  return await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id, email, name, club FROM accounts WHERE id = ?').bind(Number(accountId)).first() || null;
}

async function deleteSession(env, req) {
  const auth = req.headers.get('Authorization') || '';
  const token = auth.startsWith('Bearer ') ? auth.slice(7).trim() : null;
  if (token) await env.SESSIONS.delete(`session:${token}`);
}

// v2.55.0 — RACE-SCOPED TOTE TOKENS. The client used to open Pards-Bets.html
// with ?token=<full organizer session> — a 24-hour credential for EVERYTHING
// the organizer can do, sitting in a URL (address bar, history, any pasted
// link). Handing the tote to whoever runs the betting table meant handing
// them the whole account. A racetok:* entry is the narrow replacement: it
// names ONE race, only the four endpoints the tote calls accept it, and the
// TTL is one betting day.
const RACE_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS = 43_200; // 12 hours

// Shared access gate for the four race-scoped tote endpoints (GET race,
// POST wagers, DELETE wagers, PATCH places). Accepts EITHER a race token
// (scope check: its race_id must equal raceId — ownership was proven at mint
// time and the TTL bounds the rest) OR a full organizer session, ownership-
// checked via event/trip exactly as each handler did inline before. Returns
// { race } or { error: Response } — callers check .error first. This IS the
// auth for these endpoints — one implementation per concern, never fork a
// copy back into a handler.
async function betsRaceAccess(env, req, origin, raceId) {
  const auth = req.headers.get('Authorization') || '';
  const token = auth.startsWith('Bearer ') ? auth.slice(7).trim() : null;
  if (!token) return { error: err('Unauthorized', 401, origin) };

  const race = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT * FROM bets_races WHERE id = ?').bind(raceId).first();

  const rawScope = await env.SESSIONS.get(`racetok:${token}`);
  if (rawScope) {
    let scope = null;
    try { scope = JSON.parse(rawScope); } catch (_) { scope = null; }
    // Wrong race or corrupt entry → the same 401 a bad session gets.
    if (!scope || Number(scope.race_id) !== Number(raceId)) return { error: err('Unauthorized', 401, origin) };
    if (!race) return { error: err('Race not found', 404, origin) };
    return { race };
  }

  const account = await resolveSession(env, req);
  if (!account) return { error: err('Unauthorized', 401, origin) };
  if (!race) return { error: err('Race not found', 404, origin) };
  if (race.event_id) {
    const ev = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM events WHERE id = ? AND organizer_id = ?').bind(race.event_id, account.id).first();
    if (!ev) return { error: err('Unauthorized', 401, origin) };
  } else if (race.trip_id) {
    const tr = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM trips WHERE id = ? AND organizer_id = ?').bind(race.trip_id, account.id).first();
    if (!tr) return { error: err('Unauthorized', 401, origin) };
  }
  return { race };
}

const CODE_CHARS = '0123456789';

function generateCode() {
  const bytes = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(6));
  return Array.from(bytes).map(b => CODE_CHARS[b % CODE_CHARS.length]).join('');
}

// v2.36.0 — a league watch_code must be unique across EVERY code space the Watch
// cascade walks (events.event_code -> trips.trip_code -> leagues.watch_code).
// A collision would be silently shadowed by the earlier stage, so mint globally.
async function uniqueWatchCode(env) {
  for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) {
    const code = generateCode();
    const hit = await env.DB.prepare(
      `SELECT 1 AS x FROM events  WHERE UPPER(event_code) = ?
       UNION ALL SELECT 1 FROM trips   WHERE UPPER(trip_code)  = ?
       UNION ALL SELECT 1 FROM leagues WHERE UPPER(watch_code) = ? LIMIT 1`
    ).bind(code, code, code).first();
    if (!hit) return code;
  }
  throw new Error('Could not mint a unique watch code');
}

function generatePin() {
  const val = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(2));
  return String((val[0] * 256 + val[1]) % 9000 + 1000);
}

// ── v2.88.0 — B-70: RATING / SLOPE / PAR COME FROM D1, NOT FROM SOURCE ──────
// What used to live here: seven courses hardcoded into the worker source, and
// a single fallback tee for everything else that carried the Derrick's Deck 2
// rating, slope and par. So an unmapped course did not fail — it quietly played
// as the Derrick. Bruce, s90: remove them. They are gone; this file no longer
// contains any club's rating.
//
// D1 is the source of truth (131 courses / 801 tees) and the TRIP path has read
// it since v2.35. This is the EVENT path catching up, not a new idea.
//
// ⭐⭐ THE NEUTRAL TEE. When D1 has no usable row we refuse to borrow another
// club's rating. WHS defines slope 113 as a course of standard difficulty, so
// slope 113 with rating = par yields courseHcp = the player's Handicap Index,
// exactly — no invented difficulty in either direction. `tee_source` says
// 'neutral' so no caller can mistake it for an official rating. This is the
// v2.66.0 principle applied to ratings: we widen where we look, and where there
// is nothing honest to read we say so instead of guessing a number.
const NEUTRAL_SLOPE = 113;

function neutralTee(par) {
  const p = Number(par) > 0 ? Number(par) : 72;   // rating === par, so p only
  return { rating: p, slope: NEUTRAL_SLOPE, par: p, tee_source: 'neutral' };
}                                                 // ever cancels itself out

function _teeParsSum(v) {
  try {
    const a = typeof v === 'string' ? JSON.parse(v) : v;
    if (!Array.isArray(a) || a.length !== 18) return null;
    let t = 0;
    for (const x of a) { const n = Number(x); if (!(n > 0)) return null; t += n; }
    return t;
  } catch (_tps) { return null; }
}

async function _coursePar(env, courseId) {
  try {
    const c = await env.DB.prepare('SELECT par FROM courses WHERE id = ?').bind(courseId).first();
    return c ? _teeParsSum(c.par) : null;
  } catch (_cp) { return null; }
}

// THE one place an EVENT resolves a tee. Memoised via the optional `cache` Map
// so a per-group loop costs one query, not one per group. Never throws.
async function resolveTee(env, courseId, tee, cache) {
  const key = String(courseId || '') + '|' + String(tee || '');
  if (cache && cache.has(key)) return cache.get(key);
  let out = null;
  try {
    if (courseId && tee) {
      const t = await env.DB.prepare(
        'SELECT rating, slope, par, pars FROM course_tees WHERE course_id = ? AND name = ? COLLATE NOCASE LIMIT 1'
      ).bind(courseId, tee).first();
      if (t && Number(t.rating) > 0 && Number(t.slope) > 0) {
        let par = Number(t.par) > 0 ? Number(t.par) : null;
        if (par == null) par = _teeParsSum(t.pars);
        if (par == null) par = await _coursePar(env, courseId);
        if (par == null) par = Math.round(Number(t.rating));   // last resort: no
        out = { rating: Number(t.rating), slope: Number(t.slope),
                par, tee_source: 'course_tee' };               // par adjustment
      }
    }
    if (!out && courseId) out = neutralTee(await _coursePar(env, courseId));
  } catch (_rt) { out = null; }
  if (!out) out = neutralTee(null);
  if (cache) cache.set(key, out);
  return out;
}

function calcCourseHandicap(hdcpIndex, slope, rating, par) {
  return hdcpIndex * (slope / 113) + (rating - par);
}

async function uniqueCode(env) {
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
    const code = generateCode();
    if (!await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM groups WHERE code = ?').bind(code).first()) return code;
  }
  throw new Error('Could not generate unique code');
}

async function uniqueEventCode(env) {
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) {
    const code = generateCode();
    if (!await env.DB.prepare('SELECT id FROM events WHERE event_code = ?').bind(code).first()) return code;
  }
  throw new Error('Could not generate unique event code');
}


// ── CALLAWAY (v2.71.0) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Individual net for a field where NOBODY has a handicap index. The allowance
// is derived from the player's own finished card: total the gross, look the
// band up, deduct the N worst holes, apply the band adjustment.
//
// THREE RULES THAT ARE NOT OPTIONAL:
//   1. Holes 17 and 18 are NEVER deductible. This is what stops a player
//      blowing up the closing holes on purpose.
//   2. A hole counts at most DOUBLE PAR toward the deduction — and that needs
//      per-hole pars. eventHolePars() returns null when we genuinely do not
//      know them, and NULL IS A REAL ANSWER (see the v2.66.0 header note). We
//      do NOT invent par 4. We run without the cap and say so in `par_source`,
//      so nobody reads a capped number where none was applied.
//   3. A PARTIAL CARD HAS NO NET. Callaway is defined on a finished 18. A
//      player who quits at 12 keeps their gross, appears on the board with
//      holes_played, and carries net === null. Never a fabricated number.
const CW_BANDS = [
  { lo: 70,  hi: 72,  deduct: 0.0, scratch: true },
  { lo: 73,  hi: 75,  deduct: 0.5 },
  { lo: 76,  hi: 80,  deduct: 1.0 },
  { lo: 81,  hi: 85,  deduct: 1.5 },
  { lo: 86,  hi: 90,  deduct: 2.0 },
  { lo: 91,  hi: 95,  deduct: 2.5 },
  { lo: 96,  hi: 100, deduct: 3.0 },
  { lo: 101, hi: 105, deduct: 3.5 },
  { lo: 106, hi: 110, deduct: 4.0 },
  { lo: 111, hi: 115, deduct: 4.5 },
  { lo: 116, hi: 120, deduct: 5.0 },
  { lo: 121, hi: 125, deduct: 5.5 },
  { lo: 126, hi: 130, deduct: 6.0 },
  { lo: 131, hi: 135, deduct: 6.5 },
  { lo: 136, hi: 140, deduct: 7.0 },
  { lo: 141, hi: 145, deduct: 7.5 },
  { lo: 146, hi: 150, deduct: 8.0 }
];

function cwCallaway(holes, pars) {
  let played = 0, gross = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < 18; i++) if (holes[i] != null) { played++; gross += holes[i]; }
  if (played < 18) return { complete: false, played, gross, hcp: null, net: null, used: [] };

  let band = null;
  for (const b of CW_BANDS) if (gross >= b.lo && gross <= b.hi) { band = b; break; }
  if (!band) band = gross < 70 ? CW_BANDS[0] : CW_BANDS[CW_BANDS.length - 1];
  if (band.scratch) return { complete: true, played: 18, gross, hcp: 0, net: gross, deduct: 0, adj: 0, used: [] };

  const span = band.hi - band.lo + 1;
  const pos  = Math.min(span - 1, Math.max(0, gross - band.lo));
  const adj  = Math.round((pos / (span - 1)) * 4) - 2;

  // holes 1..16 only; capped at 2x par when — and only when — we know par
  const elig = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < 16; i++) {
    if (holes[i] == null) continue;
    const cap = pars ? pars[i] * 2 : Infinity;
    elig.push({ h: i + 1, v: Math.min(holes[i], cap) });
  }
  elig.sort((a, b) => b.v - a.v);

  const whole = Math.floor(band.deduct), half = (band.deduct % 1) > 0;
  let sum = 0; const used = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < whole && i < elig.length; i++) { sum += elig[i].v; used.push({ h: elig[i].h, v: elig[i].v, part: 1 }); }
  if (half && elig.length > whole) { const hv = elig[whole].v / 2; sum += hv; used.push({ h: elig[whole].h, v: hv, part: 0.5 }); }

  let hcp = sum + adj;
  if (hcp < 0) hcp = 0;
  if (hcp > 50) hcp = 50;
  hcp = Math.round(hcp * 10) / 10;
  return { complete: true, played: 18, gross, hcp, net: Math.round((gross - hcp) * 10) / 10, deduct: band.deduct, adj, used };
}

// PER-PLAYER standings for an individual stroke-play event.
//
// This exists because recomputeLeaderboard()'s default path is a SCRAMBLE
// best-ball producing ONE ROW PER GROUP. An individual tournament needs one
// row per PLAYER, and no amount of reading the group cache can recover it —
// the best-ball collapse throws the other three scores away.
async function computeCallawayStandings(env, event) {
  const cfg  = (() => { try { return JSON.parse(event.config || '{}'); } catch (_) { return {}; } })();
  let pars      = eventHolePars(cfg);
  let parSource = pars ? 'event_config' : 'none';
  // ⭐⭐ v2.87.0 — THE SAME FALLBACK v2.86.0 GAVE THE STABLEFORD POINTS.
  // Rule 2 in the header above (a hole counts at most DOUBLE PAR toward the
  // deduction) needs per-hole pars, and eventHolePars() reads the EVENT CONFIG
  // only — so on a config-less event the cap never applied and a 10 on a par 3
